Q1. Tens digit \(3\), digit sum \(4\): which two-digit number satisfies both facts?
A. \(31\)
B. \(32\)
C. \(30\)
D. \(33\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
3+u=4\Rightarrow u=1
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 31.

\[
N=10\times 3+1=31
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 31 is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(31\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=1
\]

\[
N=31
\]

Fast result: \(31\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_single_rule
---
Q2. If the digit sum is \(9\) and the tens digit is \(5\), what number is formed?
A. \(54\)
B. \(55\)
C. \(53\)
D. \(56\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
5+u=9\Rightarrow u=4
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 54.

\[
N=10\times 5+4=54
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 54 is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=4
\]

\[
N=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_missing_digit_multi_rule
---
Q3. The number \(936x8\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=26
\]

\[
S+x=26+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility
---
Q4. Between \(189\) and \(303\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 189 is 192.

\[
6\times 32=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 303.

\[
303=6\times 50+3
\]

\[
303-3=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{300-192}{6}+1=18+1=19
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 19.
Hence, there are 19 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(19\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{303}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{188}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
50-31=19
\]

Fast result: \(19\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_multi_condition
---
Q5. Between \(115\) and \(254\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 115 is 117.

\[
9\times 13=117
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 252 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 254.

\[
254=9\times 28+2
\]

\[
254-2=252
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{252-117}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{254}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{114}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
28-12=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q6. Between \(114\) and \(203\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 114 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 195 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 203.

\[
203=15\times 13+8
\]

\[
203-8=195
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{195-120}{15}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{203}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{113}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
13-7=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q7. For two numbers \(60\) and \(195\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(780\)
B. \(819\)
C. \(858\)
D. \(741\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
195=3\times 5\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(60,195)=\frac{60\times 195}{\operatorname{HCF}(60,195)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_divisibility_lcm_bridge

Q8. For the number \(480\), find the count of positive divisors?
A. \(23\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(25\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
480=2^{5}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the (+1) rule on exponents

Each exponent contributes one more choice than its value.

\[
d(480)=(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=24
\]

Answer Verification
The number of positive divisors is 24.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(24\).

Shortcut

Multiply (exponent + 1) across all prime powers.

\[
(5+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)
\]

Fast result: \(24\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_hidden_divisor_deduction
---

Q14. For the number \(4320\), find the count of odd divisors?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
4320=2^{5}\times 3^{3}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count odd divisors only

Ignore the power of 2; odd divisors come from odd primes only.

\[
(3+1)\times (1+1)
\]

\[
O=8
\]

Answer Verification
The number of odd divisors is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Drop the factor 2 and multiply (e+1) for remaining primes.

\[
O(n)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_odd_even_divisor_count
---
Q15. For the number \(4320\), find the sum of its positive divisors?
A. \(18144\)
B. \(13608\)
C. \(15120\)
D. \(16632\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
4320=2^{5}\times 3^{3}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(4320)=15120
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 15120.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(15120\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{4}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(15120\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_sum_of_divisors
---
Q16. For the number \(12\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(2074\)
B. \(1555\)
C. \(1728\)
D. \(1901\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
12=2^{2}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(12)=(2+1)\times (1+1)=6
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 12 d=12^{d(12)/2}=12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 1728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1728\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
12^{6/2}=1728
\]

Fast result: \(1728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_product_of_divisors
---

A. \(780\)
B. \(819\)

D. \(741\)
Answer: A

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=\frac{78\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,60)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q25. Power \(11^{39}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
11\equiv 11\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
11\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(39-1)\bmod 12+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
11^{39}\equiv 5\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(39-1)\bmod 12+1=3
\]

\[
11^{39}\equiv 5\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division
---
Q26. Power \(2^{59}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(7\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(59-1)\bmod 12+1=11
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
2^{59}\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 11th term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(59-1)\bmod 12+1=11
\]

\[
2^{59}\equiv 7\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q29. Power \(13^{42}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(4\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 4.

\[
13^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 4.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(42-1)\bmod 4+1=2
\]

\[
3^{42}\equiv 4\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q41. With tens \(3\) and ones \(6\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(27\)
B. \(28\)
C. \(26\)
D. \(29\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 3+6=36
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+3=63
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-6|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-6|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q52. Between \(155\) and \(300\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 155 is 162.

\[
9\times 18=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 297 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 300.

\[
300=9\times 33+3
\]

\[
300-3=297
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{297-162}{9}+1=15+1=16
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 16.
Hence, there are 16 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{300}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{154}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-17=16
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Fast result: \(693\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q66. By what least number should \(300\) be multiplied to make it a perfect square?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Factorize the given number

Perfect powers are controlled by prime exponents.

\[
300=2^{2}\times 3\times 5^{2}
\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[
2^{2}\to 2^{2}
\]

\[
3^{1}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{2}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=3
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q88. The number \(992x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 26; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[
\sigma(108)=280
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 280.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(280\).

Shortcut

\]

Fast result: \(280\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q92. Power \(3^{68}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(68-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 9.

\[
3^{68}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 9.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(68-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
3^{68}\equiv 9\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q97. Between \(183\) and \(320\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 183 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 320.

\[
320=15\times 21+5
\]

\[
320-5=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-195}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{320}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{182}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-12=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Hence, the number is 62.

\[
N=10\times 6+2=62
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 62 is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(62\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=2
\]

\[
N=62
\]

Fast result: \(62\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q113. How many trailing zeroes are there in \(50!\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Count powers of 5 in n!

Trailing zeroes come from factor pairs 2×5; count fives.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{5}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
10+2=12
\]

Answer Verification
Trailing zeroes in 50! = 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Sum only ⌊n/5⌋ + ⌊n/25⌋ + …

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{50}{5}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{50}{25}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q114. For exponent \(117\), the powers of \(17\) repeat in remainders modulo \(13\). What remainder appears?
A. \(14\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
17\equiv 4\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(117-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 12.

\[
17^{117}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(117-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
4^{117}\equiv 12\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q120. Between \(192\) and \(297\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 192 is 198.

\[
18\times 11=198
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 288 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 297.

\[
297=18\times 16+9
\]

\[
297-9=288
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{288-198}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{297}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{191}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-10=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q121. Two buses leave a stand every \(40\) and \(80\) minutes. When will they next leave together?
A. \(82\)
B. \(80\)
C. \(81\)
D. \(79\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
40=2^{3}\times 5
\]

\[
80=2^{4}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=80
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 80.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(80\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(40,80)=\frac{40\times 80}{\operatorname{HCF}(40,80)}=80
\]

Fast result: \(80\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 216.

\[
8^{56}\equiv 216\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 8th term of the cycle is 216.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(856\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(56-1)\bmod 24+1=8
\]

\[
8^{56}\equiv 216\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(856\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q149. The number \(3x216\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q150. Bells ring at intervals of \(75\) and \(90\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(495\)
B. \(427\)
C. \(450\)
D. \(473\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
75=3\times 5^{2}
\]

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,90)=450
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 450.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(450\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(75,90)=\frac{75\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(75,90)}=450
\]

Fast result: \(450\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q151. After prime factorising \(24\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(364954\)
B. \(414720\)
C. \(497664\)
D. \(331776\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
24=2^{3}\times 3
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(24)=(3+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 24 d=24^{d(24)/2}=24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 331776.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(331776\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
24^{8/2}=331776
\]

Fast result: \(331776\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q167. When \(2^{64}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(64-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
2^{64}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(64-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{64}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q178. The number \(510x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q179. For two numbers \(165\) and \(105\), which common multiple is first reached?
A. \(1155\)
B. \(1271\)
C. \(1386\)
D. \(1039\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
165=3\times 5\times 11
\]

\[
105=3\times 5\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,105)=1155
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1155.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1155\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(165,105)=\frac{165\times 105}{\operatorname{HCF}(165,105)}=1155
\]

Fast result: \(1155\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q180. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the product of all positive divisors?
A. \(11085466\)
B. \(12597120\)
C. \(15116544\)
D. \(10077696\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(36)=(2+1)\times (2+1)=9
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 36 d=36^{d(36)/2}=36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 10077696.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10077696\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
36^{9/2}=10077696
\]

Fast result: \(10077696\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(38\)
B. \(36\)
C. \(37\)
D. \(35\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+2=62
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+6=26
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-2|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-2|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q193. Between \(127\) and \(305\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(28\)
B. \(31\)
C. \(29\)
D. \(30\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 127 is 132.

\[
6\times 22=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 300 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 305.

\[
305=6\times 50+5
\]

\[
305-5=300
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{300-132}{6}+1=28+1=29
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 29.
Hence, there are 29 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(29\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{305}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{126}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
50-21=29
\]

Fast result: \(29\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q194. Bells ring at intervals of \(30\) and \(36\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(198\)
B. \(171\)
C. \(180\)
D. \(189\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(30,36)=180
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 180.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(180\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(30,36)=\frac{30\times 36}{\operatorname{HCF}(30,36)}=180
\]

Fast result: \(180\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_hcf_lcm_relation

Q196. Power \(13^{35}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(10\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(12\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(35-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 10.

\[
13^{35}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 5th term of the cycle is 10.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(35-1)\bmod 10+1=5
\]

\[
2^{35}\equiv 10\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_remainder_after_division

Q198. Digit sum \(16\) with tens digit \(8\) identifies which two-digit number?
A. \(90\)
B. \(88\)
C. \(89\)
D. \(87\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Form the digit-sum equation

\[
8+u=16\Rightarrow u=8
\]

Step 2: Write the two-digit number

Hence, the number is 88.

\[
N=10\times 8+8=88
\]

Answer Verification
Digit sum of 88 is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(88\).

Shortcut

Find the ones digit from the digit sum, then place it.

\[
u=8
\]

\[
N=88
\]

Fast result: \(88\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q205. Between \(222\) and \(308\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(12\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(13\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 222 is 224.

\[
8\times 28=224
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 304 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 308.

\[
308=8\times 38+4
\]

\[
308-4=304
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{304-224}{8}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{308}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{221}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
38-27=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q210. Number \(66\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+6=66
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+6=66
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-6|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-6|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q217. The number \(514x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q224. A number leaves the same remainder as \(7^{109}\) when divided by \(19\). What is that remainder?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 3.

\[
7\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(109-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 7.

\[
7^{109}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 7.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(109-1)\bmod 3+1=1
\]

\[
7^{109}\equiv 7\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q230. Between \(199\) and \(339\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 199 is 200.

\[
8\times 25=200
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 336 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 339.

\[
339=8\times 42+3
\]

\[
339-3=336
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{336-200}{8}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{339}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{198}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
42-24=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q231. After prime factorising \(36\), what is the sum of all positive divisors?
A. \(92\)
B. \(90\)
C. \(93\)
D. \(91\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
36=2^{2}\times 3^{2}
\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(36)=91
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 91.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(91\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{3}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}
\]

Fast result: \(91\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(104,56)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

(71-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{71}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(71-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

\[
3^{71}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q254. Between \(116\) and \(237\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 116 is 120.

\[
15\times 8=120
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 225 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 237.

\[
237=15\times 15+12
\]

\[
237-12=225
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{225-120}{15}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{237}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{115}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
15-7=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(900\).

Fill only the missing prime powers; do not search through squares or cubes.

\[
M=3
\]

Fast result: \(900\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(65-1)\bmod 3+1=2
\]

\[
7^{65}\equiv 11\pmod{19}
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q281. Between \(224\) and \(384\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 224 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 378 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 384.

\[
384=18\times 21+6
\]

\[
384-6=378
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{378-234}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{384}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{223}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-12=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 5.

\[
3\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(96-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
3^{96}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(96-1)\bmod 5+1=1
\]

\[
3^{96}\equiv 3\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q292. The number \(9x650\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q306. Between \(179\) and \(268\), how many integers are divisible by \(8\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 179 is 184.

\[
8\times 23=184
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 268.

\[
268=8\times 33+4
\]

\[
268-4=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{264-184}{8}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{268}{8}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{178}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
33-22=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q308. When \(3^{57}\) is divided by \(7\), what remainder is left?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(57-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
3^{57}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(57-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[
3^{57}\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q333. Between \(234\) and \(374\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 234 is 240.

\[
15\times 16=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 374.

\[
374=15\times 24+14
\]

\[
374-14=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{360-240}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{374}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{233}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
24-15=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q334. Two cyclic events repeat every \(234\) and \(126\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(1802\)
B. \(1966\)
C. \(1474\)
D. \(1638\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
234=2\times 3^{2}\times 13
\]

\[
126=2\times 3^{2}\times 7
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(234,126)=1638
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 1638.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1638\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(234,126)=\frac{234\times 126}{\operatorname{HCF}(234,126)}=1638
\]

Fast result: \(1638\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q335. Power \(3^{34}\) is tested modulo \(9\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(0\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
3\rightarrow 0
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(34-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 0.

\[
3^{34}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(34-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
3^{34}\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(26\)
B. \(29\)

Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[

\]

\[
N_2=10\times 2+5=25
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-2|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-2|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Therefore, 228 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 228.

\[
228=6\times 38+0
\]

\[
228-0=228
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{228-150}{6}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{228}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{147}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
38-24=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q360. When \(2^{39}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(5\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(39-1)\bmod 10+1=9
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
2^{39}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 9th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(39-1)\bmod 10+1=9
\]

\[
2^{39}\equiv 6\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(45\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

N=1728

Final Answer

\[

Q370. The number \(26x41\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-7
\]

\[
-7+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-7+x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-7+7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

The LCM is 462.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(462\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(66,42)=\frac{66\times 42}{\operatorname{HCF}(66,42)}=462
\]

Fast result: \(462\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q386. When \(7^{72}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(0\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(72-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{72}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(72-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{72}\equiv 1\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q389. Between \(104\) and \(185\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 104 is 108.

\[
9\times 12=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 180 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 185.

\[
185=9\times 20+5
\]

\[
185-5=180
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{180-108}{9}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{185}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{103}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
20-11=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

\[
2^{6}\to 2^{6}
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[
x=9
\]

Answer Verification

\[
N=9
\]

Final Answer

Shortcut

\[
M=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 6+3=63
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+6=36
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |6-3|=9\times 3=27
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 27.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |6-3|=27
\]

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_digit_interchange

Q414. Power \(3^{36}\) is tested modulo \(7\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
3\equiv 3\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[
3\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(36-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
3^{36}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 6th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(36-1)\bmod 6+1=6
\]

\[
3^{36}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q419. The number \(910x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 16.

\[
16+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
16+x=21
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
16+5=21
\]
\[
21\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q420. Two cyclic events repeat every \(48\) and \(120\) minutes. After how many minutes will they occur together again?
A. \(252\)
B. \(264\)
C. \(228\)
D. \(240\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
48=2^{4}\times 3
\]

\[
120=2^{3}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,120)=240
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 240.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(240\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(48,120)=\frac{48\times 120}{\operatorname{HCF}(48,120)}=240
\]

Fast result: \(240\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(15\)
B. \(13\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: D

Therefore, the first multiple of 8 greater than 117 is 120.

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 224 is the greatest multiple of 8 not exceeding 229.

\[
229=8\times 28+5
\]

\[
229-5=224
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 8.

\[
\frac{224-120}{8}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 8 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(14\).

\]

\[

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q436. The number \(7x872\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 24; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
24+x=27
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
24+3=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q448. Between \(116\) and \(199\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 116 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 198 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 199.

\[
199=18\times 11+1
\]

\[
199-1=198
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{198-126}{18}+1=4+1=5
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 5.
Hence, there are 5 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{199}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{115}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
11-6=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Therefore, 270 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 282.

\[
282=15\times 18+12
\]

\[
282-12=270
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{270-180}{15}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{282}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{166}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
18-11=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q469. The number \(69x16\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q479. The number \(510x8\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+8=48
\]

\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
48\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+8\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

\]

\]

\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification

\[
10+1=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Shortcut

\[

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q498. Between \(100\) and \(261\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 100 is 108.

\[
9\times 12=108
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 261 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 261.

\[
261=9\times 29+0
\]

\[
261-0=261
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{261-108}{9}+1=17+1=18
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 18.
Hence, there are 18 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{261}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{99}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
29-11=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

A. \(17\)
B. \(20\)
C. \(18\)
D. \(19\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+9=79
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+7=97
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-9|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-9|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q509. The number \(84x27\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=9
\]

\[
9+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
9+x=11
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
9+2=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q518. Between \(161\) and \(321\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(8\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 161 is 162.

\[
18\times 9=162
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 306 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 321.

\[
321=18\times 17+15
\]

\[
321-15=306
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{306-162}{18}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{321}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{160}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
17-8=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q519. Power \(13^{23}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(2\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(1\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(23-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 2.

\[
13^{23}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 2.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(23-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
3^{23}\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 2: Complete the exponents

For a square, every exponent must be even.

\[

\]

\[
3^{2}\to 3^{2}
\]

\[
5^{1}\to 5^{2}
\]

Step 3: Read the required value

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The perfect-power condition is now satisfied.
\[
N=900
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(900\).

\[

\]

Fast result: \(900\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

C. \(2\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |3-3|=9\times 0=0
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 0.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |3-3|=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[
963=36\times 26+27
\]

\[
x=27
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 27.
\[
N=27
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(27\).

Fast result: \(27\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q546. The number \(536x8\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=22
\]

\[
S+x=22+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q547. For exponent \(72\), what ending digit does \(2^{72}\) have?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(72-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 6.

\[
2^{72}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 6.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(72-1)\bmod 4+1=4
\]

\[
2^{72}\equiv 6\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(11\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 195 is 204.

\[
12\times 17=204
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 312 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 317.

\[
317=12\times 26+5
\]

\[
317-5=312
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{312-204}{12}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{317}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{194}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
26-16=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Answer: B

Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 6.

\[

\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[

\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 12.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 12.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(12\).

\[
p=(51-1)\bmod 6+1=3
\]

\[

Fast result: \(12\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q576. The number \(232x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(3\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 15; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\[

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(90,234)=1170
\]

Answer Verification

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1170\).

Shortcut

\[

\]

Fast result: \(1170\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

C. \(3\)
D. \(6\)

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(94-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
13^{94}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(94-1)\bmod 10+1=4
\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

C. \(7\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: A

Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=19
\]

\[
S+x=19+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Answer: C

\[

\]

Step 2: Apply the sum-of-divisors rule

Multiply the geometric-sum factor for each prime power.

\[
\sigma(1440)=4914
\]

Answer Verification
The sum of all positive divisors is 4914.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4914\).

Shortcut

Write prime powers once, then multiply the σ-factors.

\[
\frac{2^{6}-1}{2-1}\times \frac{3^{3}-1}{3-1}\times \frac{5^{2}-1}{5-1}
\]

Fast result: \(4914\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q613. The number \(236x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 11.

\[
11+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
11+x=15
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
11+4=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q616. Between \(191\) and \(316\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 191 is 195.

\[
15\times 13=195
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 315 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 316.

\[
316=15\times 21+1
\]

\[
316-1=315
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{315-195}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{316}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{190}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
21-12=9
\]

Fast result: \(9\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q624. Cycle position \(116\) is needed for powers of \(19\) modulo \(17\). What is the remainder?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(116-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 16.

\[
19^{116}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(116-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

\[
2^{116}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Family: ns_multi_cluster_reasoning
---

Q627. The number \(4x650\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=15
\]

\[
S+x=15+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
15+x=18
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=3\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
15+3=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q634. The number \(10x85\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(2\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=-2
\]

\[
-2+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
-2+x=0
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
-2+2=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\]

\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=2244
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 2244.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2244\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(132,204)=\frac{132\times 204}{\operatorname{HCF}(132,204)}=2244
\]

Fast result: \(2244\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q641. The number \(332x0\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 8; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
8+x=9
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
8+1=9
\]
\[
9\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Answer Verification

\]

Fast result: \(28\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q653. Between \(229\) and \(326\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(10\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 229 is 240.

\[
12\times 20=240
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 326.

\[
326=12\times 27+2
\]

\[
326-2=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{324-240}{12}+1=7+1=8
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{326}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{228}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
27-19=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q654. Power \(2^{76}\) is tested modulo \(13\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{13}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(76-1)\bmod 12+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
2^{76}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(76-1)\bmod 12+1=4
\]

\[
2^{76}\equiv 3\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q671. The number \(758x2\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(5\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=22
\]

\[
S+x=22+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).
