Q680. Between \(120\) and \(218\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 120 is 126.

\[
18\times 7=126
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 216 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 218.

\[
218=18\times 12+2
\]

\[
218-2=216
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{216-126}{18}+1=5+1=6
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 6.
Hence, there are 6 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{218}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{119}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
12-6=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q681. Cycle position \(36\) is needed for powers of \(19\) modulo \(17\). What is the remainder?
A. \(16\)
B. \(17\)
C. \(15\)
D. \(18\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
19\equiv 2\pmod{17}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 8.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 16\rightarrow 15\rightarrow 13\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(36-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 16.

\[
19^{36}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Answer Verification
The 4th term of the cycle is 16.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(16\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(36-1)\bmod 8+1=4
\]

\[
2^{36}\equiv 16\pmod{17}
\]

Fast result: \(16\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\]

\[
x=3
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[

\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 3\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=3
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(2\)
B. \(0\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=12
\]

\[
12-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
12-x=11
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
12-1=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q706. Between \(182\) and \(362\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(17\)
B. \(15\)
C. \(16\)
D. \(14\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 182 is 192.

\[
12\times 16=192
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 360 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 362.

\[
362=12\times 30+2
\]

\[
362-2=360
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{360-192}{12}+1=14+1=15
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 15.
Hence, there are 15 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(15\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{362}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{181}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
30-15=15
\]

Fast result: \(15\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q707. Power \(13^{43}\) is tested modulo \(11\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(8\)
B. \(9\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 2\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 7\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(43-1)\bmod 10+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
13^{43}\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(43-1)\bmod 10+1=3
\]

\[
2^{43}\equiv 8\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q714. The number \(3x129\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=11
\]

\[
11-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
11-x=11
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=0\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
11-0=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=0
\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

A. \(1089\)
B. \(940\)
C. \(990\)
D. \(1040\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
99=3^{2}\times 11
\]

\[
90=2\times 3^{2}\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=990
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 990.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(990\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(99,90)=\frac{99\times 90}{\operatorname{HCF}(99,90)}=990
\]

Fast result: \(990\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q724. The number \(292x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(8\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 19; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
19+x=27
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=8\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
19+8=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 8\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q734. The number \(43x10\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(1\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(0\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 8; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
8+x=9
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
8+1=9
\]
\[
9\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q735. When \(7^{42}\) is divided by \(11\), what remainder is left?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
7\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 10.

\[
7\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(42-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 5.

\[
7^{42}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 5.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(42-1)\bmod 10+1=2
\]

\[
7^{42}\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q746. Between \(137\) and \(264\), how many integers are divisible by \(12\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(11\)
C. \(12\)
D. \(10\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 12 greater than 137 is 144.

\[
12\times 12=144
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 264 is the greatest multiple of 12 not exceeding 264.

\[
264=12\times 22+0
\]

\[
264-0=264
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 12.

\[
\frac{264-144}{12}+1=10+1=11
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 11.
Hence, there are 11 multiples of 12 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(11\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{264}{12}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{136}{12}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
22-11=11
\]

Fast result: \(11\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

C. \(3\)

Answer: A

Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=23
\]

\[
S+x=23+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
23+x=27
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
23+4=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q757. The power \(12^{119}\) is very large. What are its final digit?
A. \(7\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(9\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
12\equiv 2\pmod{10}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 6
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(119-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 8.

\[
12^{119}\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 8.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(119-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{119}\equiv 8\pmod{10}
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[

Fast result: \(43\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q760. Between \(216\) and \(392\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(19\)
B. \(22\)
C. \(20\)
D. \(21\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 216 is 216.

\[
9\times 24=216
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 387 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 392.

\[
392=9\times 43+5
\]

\[
392-5=387
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{387-216}{9}+1=19+1=20
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 20.
Hence, there are 20 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(20\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{392}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{215}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
43-23=20
\]

Fast result: \(20\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q764. The number \(592x6\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(7\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 6, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 22; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
22+x=27
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
22+5=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
6\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q767. With tens \(5\) and ones \(9\), a two-digit number is written. What difference appears after reversing?
A. \(36\)
B. \(37\)
C. \(35\)
D. \(38\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 5+9=59
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 9+5=95
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |5-9|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |5-9|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

This makes the final candidate satisfy the full chain, not only the first clue.

\[
A=3
\]

\[
N=3
\]

Final Answer

Q771. The number \(898x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 31.

\[
31+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
31+x=36
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
31+5=36
\]
\[
36\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q788. The number \(970x4\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 20.

\[
20+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+4=44
\]

\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
20+x=24
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
20+4=24
\]
\[
24\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
44\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+4\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(801\)
C. \(692\)
D. \(728\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
56=2^{3}\times 7
\]

\[
104=2^{3}\times 13
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=728
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 728.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(728\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(56,104)=\frac{56\times 104}{\operatorname{HCF}(56,104)}=728
\]

Fast result: \(728\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

\[

\]

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
11+x=18
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
11+7=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[

\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q808. Between \(213\) and \(354\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(10\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 213 is 225.

\[
15\times 15=225
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 354.

\[
354=15\times 23+9
\]

\[
354-9=345
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{345-225}{15}+1=8+1=9
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 9.
Hence, there are 9 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(9\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[

Q812. The number \(332x8\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

D. \(3\)
Answer: A

\[
13\equiv 6\pmod{7}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 2.

\[
6\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(24-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
13^{24}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Answer Verification
The 2nd term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(24-1)\bmod 2+1=2
\]

\[
6^{24}\equiv 1\pmod{7}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q829. The number \(332x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 8.

\[
8+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
8+x=12
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
8+4=12
\]
\[
12\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

B. \(1440\)
C. \(1584\)
D. \(1728\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Combine the number conditions first

Optimization is done only after the divisibility or remainder condition is converted into one usable cycle.

\[
m=120
\]

Step 2: Move to the nearest valid boundary

The boundary step prevents picking a number that satisfies only the arithmetic but not the given range.

\[
q=\left\lceil\frac{1389}{120}\right\rceil
\]

\[
N=120\times q=1440
\]

Answer Verification
The optimized valid value is 1440.
\[
N=1440
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(1440\).

Shortcut

Use the combined cycle and take only the needed boundary correction.

\[
Z=1440
\]

Fast result: \(1440\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q836. The number \(1x438\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(1\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 8, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 16; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
16+x=18
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=2\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
16+2=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
8\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(2\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 2\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=2
\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q837. Power \(13^{97}\) is tested modulo \(5\). Which remainder is obtained?
A. \(3\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(2\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
13\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
3\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(97-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
13^{97}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 1st term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(97-1)\bmod 4+1=1
\]

\[
3^{97}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q839. Tens digit \(7\) and ones digit \(1\) are used in a number. How much does it change when reversed?
A. \(53\)
B. \(56\)
C. \(54\)
D. \(55\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+1=71
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 1+7=17
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-1|=9\times 6=54
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 54.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(54\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-1|=54
\]

Fast result: \(54\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{8}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Step 2: Add the quotient terms

\[
15+7+3+1=26
\]

Answer Verification
Highest power of 2 dividing 30! is 26.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(26\).

Shortcut

Legendre sum of floor quotients only.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{30}{2}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{4}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{8}\right\rfloor+\left\lfloor\frac{30}{16}\right\rfloor
\]

Fast result: \(26\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q842. Between \(129\) and \(280\), how many integers are divisible by \(6\)?
A. \(25\)
B. \(26\)
C. \(24\)
D. \(27\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 6 greater than 129 is 132.

\[
6\times 22=132
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 276 is the greatest multiple of 6 not exceeding 280.

\[
280=6\times 46+4
\]

\[
280-4=276
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 6.

\[
\frac{276-132}{6}+1=24+1=25
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 25.
Hence, there are 25 multiples of 6 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(25\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{280}{6}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{128}{6}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
46-21=25
\]

Fast result: \(25\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q846. The number \(71x36\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=17
\]

\[
S+x=17+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 18, a multiple of 9.

\[
17+x=18
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
17+1=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Complete the known digit sum to the next multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 1\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q853. The number \(341x7\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(9\)
D. \(7\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=7
\]

\[
7-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
7-x=0
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
7-7=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q866. Between \(204\) and \(329\), how many integers are divisible by \(9\)?
A. \(13\)
B. \(16\)
C. \(14\)
D. \(15\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 9 greater than 204 is 207.

\[
9\times 23=207
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 324 is the greatest multiple of 9 not exceeding 329.

\[
329=9\times 36+5
\]

\[
329-5=324
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 9.

\[
\frac{324-207}{9}+1=13+1=14
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 14.
Hence, there are 14 multiples of 9 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(14\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{329}{9}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{203}{9}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
36-22=14
\]

Fast result: \(14\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q870. The number \(354x2\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 2, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 14; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
2\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(528\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

\[

\]

\[

\[

Final Answer

\[

\]

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

\]

Final Answer

\]

Fast result: \(78\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

\[

Fast result: \(2\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q904. The number \(310x6\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(4\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(5\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 10.

\[
10+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+6=56
\]

\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 5.

\[
10+x=15
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
10+5=15
\]
\[
15\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
56\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+6\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\[
8\rightarrow 64\rightarrow 512\rightarrow 96\rightarrow 768\rightarrow 144\rightarrow 152\rightarrow 216\rightarrow 728\rightarrow 824\rightarrow 592\rightarrow 736\rightarrow 888\rightarrow 104\rightarrow 832\rightarrow 656\rightarrow 248\rightarrow 984\rightarrow 872\rightarrow 976\rightarrow 808\rightarrow 464\rightarrow 712\rightarrow 696

Hence, remainder = 872.

\[
8^{115}\equiv 872\pmod{1000}
\]

Answer Verification
The 19th term of the cycle is 872.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(832\).

Shortcut

\[
p=(115-1)\bmod 24+1=19
\]

\[
8^{115}\equiv 872\pmod{1000}
\]

Fast result: \(832\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q917. Between \(102\) and \(244\), how many integers are divisible by \(15\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(12\)
C. \(10\)
D. \(11\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 15 greater than 102 is 105.

\[
15\times 7=105
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 240 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 244.

\[
244=15\times 16+4
\]

\[
244-4=240
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[
\frac{240-105}{15}+1=9+1=10
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 10.
Hence, there are 10 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(10\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{244}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{101}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
16-6=10
\]

Fast result: \(10\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Fast result: \(13\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q931. The number \(32x05\) is divisible by \(11\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(6\)
B. \(4\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: D

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the alternating-sum rule for 11

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=6
\]

\[
6+x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 11.

\[
6+x=11
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=5\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
6+5=11
\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(5\).

Shortcut

Write the alternate-sum expression and make it a multiple of 11.

\[
x\equiv 5\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=5
\]

Fast result: \(5\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q932. Bells ring at intervals of \(78\) and \(60\) minutes. After how many minutes will both ring together again?
A. \(858\)
B. \(741\)
C. \(780\)
D. \(819\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorizations

\[
78=2\times 3\times 13
\]

\[
60=2^{2}\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Take highest powers for LCM

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=780
\]

Answer Verification
The LCM is 780.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(780\).

Shortcut

Use the product identity with the HCF.

\[
\operatorname{LCM}(78,60)=\frac{78\times 60}{\operatorname{HCF}(78,60)}=780
\]

Fast result: \(780\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q933. For the number \(30\), find the product of its positive divisors?
A. \(1012500\)
B. \(1215000\)
C. \(810000\)
D. \(891000\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write prime factorization

\[
30=2\times 3\times 5
\]

Step 2: Count the divisors

\[
d(30)=(1+1)\times (1+1)\times (1+1)=8
\]

Step 3: Use the product-of-divisors identity

\[
\prod d\mid 30 d=30^{d(30)/2}=30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Answer Verification
The product of all positive divisors is 810000.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(810000\).

Shortcut

After counting divisors, raise n to half the divisor count.

\[
30^{8/2}=810000
\]

Fast result: \(810000\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q941. The number \(87x92\) is divisible by \(9\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(1\)
B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Use the divisibility rule for 9

A number is divisible by 9 when the sum of its digits is a multiple of 9.

\[
S=26
\]

\[
S+x=26+x
\]

Step 2: Form and solve the digit-sum equation

The completed digit sum must be 27, a multiple of 9.

\[
26+x=27
\]

\[
x=1
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=1\), the digit-sum rule is satisfied.
\[
26+1=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

C. \(6\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: D

Explanation:

For divisibility by 11, the difference of alternate digit sums must be a multiple of 11.

\[
A=4
\]

\[
4-x\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

The valid multiple of 11 for this pattern is 0.

\[
4-x=0
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the alternate digit-sum difference is a multiple of 11.
\[
4-4=0
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{11}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Q955. Between \(217\) and \(343\), how many integers are divisible by \(18\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Find the first valid multiple

Therefore, the first multiple of 18 greater than 217 is 234.

\[
18\times 13=234
\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 342 is the greatest multiple of 18 not exceeding 343.

\[
343=18\times 19+1
\]

\[
343-1=342
\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 18.

\[
\frac{342-234}{18}+1=6+1=7
\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 7.
Hence, there are 7 multiples of 18 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Count multiples using quotient floors on the range limits.

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{343}{18}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{216}{18}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
19-12=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q965. The number \(1x650\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(8\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(7\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 12; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
12+x=18
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=6\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
12+6=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(6\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 6\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=6
\]

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

\[

\]

Step 2: Find the last valid multiple

Therefore, 345 is the greatest multiple of 15 not exceeding 352.

\[
352=15\times 23+7
\]

\[

\]

Step 3: Count the valid multiples

The multiples form an arithmetic progression with common difference 15.

\[

\]

Answer Verification
The total number of multiples is 8.
Hence, there are 8 multiples of 15 in the given range.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(8\).

Shortcut

\[
\left\lfloor\frac{352}{15}\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor\frac{232}{15}\right\rfloor
\]

\[
23-15=8
\]

Fast result: \(8\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

Q972. The number \(85x70\) is divisible by \(18\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(9\)
B. \(7\)
C. \(8\)
D. \(6\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 18

A number divisible by 18 must be even and must also be divisible by 9.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]

Step 2: Check the even condition

The last digit is 0, so the even condition is satisfied.

\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Step 3: Use the digit-sum rule for 9

The known digit sum is 20; make the full sum a multiple of 9.

\[
20+x=27
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=7\), both tests for 18 are satisfied.
\[
20+7=27
\]
\[
27\equiv 0\pmod{9}
\]
\[
0\equiv 0\pmod{2}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(7\).

Shortcut

Evenness is fixed by the last digit; complete the digit sum to a multiple of 9.

\[
x\equiv 7\pmod{9}
\]

\[
x=7
\]

Fast result: \(7\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

B. \(2\)
C. \(0\)
D. \(3\)
Answer: A

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[

\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 12.

\[
7\rightarrow 10\rightarrow 5\rightarrow 9\rightarrow 11\rightarrow 12\rightarrow 6\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 8\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 2\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(84-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 1.

\[
7^{84}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Answer Verification
The 12th term of the cycle is 1.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A). Computed value: \(1\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(84-1)\bmod 12+1=12
\]

\[
7^{84}\equiv 1\pmod{13}
\]

Fast result: \(1\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (A).

A. \(35\)

D. \(37\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 7+3=73
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 3+7=37
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |7-3|=9\times 4=36
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 36.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(36\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |7-3|=36
\]

Fast result: \(36\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

\]

Step 2: Solve the alternating-sum equation

\]

\[
x=0
\]

Answer Verification

\[

\]
\[
11\equiv 0\pmod{11}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D). Computed value: \(0\).

Shortcut

\]

\[

\]

Fast result: \(0\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (D).

Fast result: \(6\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).

Q996. The number \(158x0\) is divisible by \(12\). What is the value of \(x\)?
A. \(3\)
B. \(6\)
C. \(4\)
D. \(5\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Split the rule for 12

A number divisible by 12 must be divisible by both 3 and 4.

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

\[
N\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 2: Use the digit sum for divisibility by 3

The known digit sum is 14.

\[
14+x\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]

Step 3: Use the last-two-digits rule for 4

The last two digits must form a number divisible by 4.

\[
10x+0=40
\]

\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Step 4: Combine both conditions

The digit satisfying both tests is 4.

\[
14+x=18
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Answer Verification
With \(x=4\), the tests for 3 and 4 are both satisfied.
\[
14+4=18
\]
\[
18\equiv 0\pmod{3}
\]
\[
40\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(4\).

Shortcut

Check 3 from the digit sum and 4 from the last two digits.

\[
x\equiv 4\pmod{3}
\]

\[
10x+0\equiv 0\pmod{4}
\]

\[
x=4
\]

Fast result: \(4\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Family: ns_reverse_divisibility

Q997. When \(2^{43}\) is divided by \(5\), what remainder is left?
A. \(2\)
B. \(5\)
C. \(3\)
D. \(4\)
Answer: C

Explanation:
Step 1: Reduce the base modulo the divisor

\[
2\equiv 2\pmod{5}
\]

Step 2: Write the remainder cycle

Cycle length = 4.

\[
2\rightarrow 4\rightarrow 3\rightarrow 1
\]

Step 3: Locate the exponent position in the cycle

\[
(43-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

Step 4: Read the required remainder

Hence, remainder = 3.

\[
2^{43}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Answer Verification
The 3rd term of the cycle is 3.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C). Computed value: \(3\).

Shortcut

Reduce the base once, then jump to the exponent position in the cycle.

\[
p=(43-1)\bmod 4+1=3
\]

\[
2^{43}\equiv 3\pmod{5}
\]

Fast result: \(3\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (C).

Q999. Number \(86\) is reversed by interchanging its digits. What absolute difference is obtained?
A. \(20\)
B. \(18\)
C. \(19\)
D. \(17\)
Answer: B

Explanation:
Step 1: Write both numbers by place value

\[
N_1=10\times 8+6=86
\]

\[
N_2=10\times 6+8=68
\]

Step 2: Subtract using the digit-difference rule

\[
|N_1-N_2|=9\times |8-6|=9\times 2=18
\]

Answer Verification
The required difference is 18.

Final Answer
Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B). Computed value: \(18\).

Shortcut

Reversal difference equals nine times the digit difference.

\[
9\times |8-6|=18
\]

Fast result: \(18\). Therefore, the correct answer is Option (B).
